With no `operations`, a document of at most `openapi_max_tools` (40) operations gets its first `openapi_tools` (8) non-deprecated reads; a bigger one returns the listing (counts by tag, 50 a page, `tag` and `search` filters) and writes nothing. That is how a 500-operation API is handled: by choosing, never one tool each. Names, operationIds, `"GET /path"` and `"tag:<tag>"` select; more than the max is refused with a hint to split by tag. Operations needing a file upload, a required cookie or a non-JSON, non-form body are listed as unsupported and can't be chosen.
- **What a tool gets**: params from path, query and header parameters plus `body` (JSON or form; renamed if a parameter is called `body`); GET/HEAD `read`, DELETE `destructive`, others `write`; GET/HEAD/PUT/DELETE `idempotent`. **Reads are `untrusted`**: the generator can't tell an API's own data from third-party text, and taint is the safe side; the result says to remove it where results are only the user's own data. The shared request code in the file builds paths with percent-encoded values (a value with `/` is then refused by the path allowlist, which rejects `%2f`), repeats or comma-joins query arrays per the document's style, and returns the JSON or body, erroring with the status and the first 500 bytes otherwise.
- **Allowlists** are the server's host (no port: entries can't carry one) with each chosen operation's full path, `{x}` as `*`. A trailing `*` matches the rest of the path (T4b-i's glob), so `/pets/*` also allows `/pets/1/x`; tightening that would change every connector's allowlist, so it's left. More than 40 entries, or a path an entry can't express, collapse to `host/base/*`.
- **Auth** comes from the schemes the chosen operations name, best first: OAuth 2 with a sign-in flow (authorization code or implicit, run as code + PKCE) → `capabilities.oauth = { <provider> = { scopes = … } }` with only the scopes those operations need, the provider being a built-in profile when the title or host matches one, else `provider_name(authorizeUrl)` (as T4a names discovered issuers); OpenID Connect likewise; an API key in a header or query → `{ secret = "<NAME>_API_KEY", header|query = … }`; bearer, or OAuth with only client-credentials/password flows → `"<NAME>_TOKEN"`; HTTP Basic → two secrets. Cookie keys and other HTTP schemes add nothing and say so. For OAuth not covered by a profile, the result carries a ready researched plan (authorize and token URLs, API host, scopes, the document URL as evidence) for `auth_propose` if `auth_discover` finds nothing; the OAuth-first order stays the agent's (discover, propose, connect), so the document never picks for the user. Never `ctx.secret`.
- **Rate**: an `x-rate-limit`/`x-ratelimit`/`x-rateLimit`/`x-rate-limits` extension on the root or `info` (`"60/min"`, a number per minute, or `{ limit, period }`), else "N requests per <unit>" in `info.description`. Nothing stated, nothing declared (the host default applies).
- **Fixtures** (`tern_lua::fixtures`): `<name>.fixtures.json` beside `<name>.luau`, a versioned JSON of cases (tool, args, result, HTTP exchanges). **This is the path bundles should carry** (T4b-iii): it sits next to the connector in `connectors/` or `connectors/drafts/`, and `connector_activate` moves a draft's fixtures with it.
- **Recording happens in `ctx.http`** through a tape on the call (`Vm::call_taped`): the request is captured before credentials are injected (so `auth` headers and query keys never reach it), then every known secret value is scrubbed, credential-like request headers are dropped, response headers are kept only from a short list (content type, paging, rate limits, caching, location), and JSON fields and query parameters named like credentials have their *string* values redacted (`access_token`, `api_key`, `password`, `client_secret`, `session…`; not `next_page_token`, `cursor`, or a numeric `tokens`). Bodies are cut at 256 KB. A case's `result` is what replaying its recording gives, so later replays compare like with like.
- **Replay** answers each request from the tape by method, URL (query order ignored) and body (JSON compared as JSON), before the rate limiter and before any secret is looked up, so it needs neither network nor secrets, and `ctx.kv` is a fresh in-memory store, so a replay is repeatable and never moves a real cursor or counter; a request that wasn't recorded fails with "no recorded response". It's the same `ctx.http` path, so allowlist and read-only checks still apply.
- **`connector_test` modes**: `auto` (default) replays when a case for these exact arguments exists, else calls live and records (only when the call made a request); `live` calls and re-records; `replay` never touches the network. A replay needs **no approval** (nothing leaves the machine), except for a legacy connector that reads raw secrets, which could still return one. The policy's decision is remembered per call (`Inspected`), so a fixture deleted between approval and call can't turn an unapproved replay into a live request. `[connectors] record_tests = false` turns recording off.
- **CLI**: `tern connectors test <name>` replays every case and exits non-zero on an error or a changed result (CI); `--record` re-records the recorded *read* calls, or every read tool that needs no arguments, against the live API with the real store and accounts (OAuth connectors run signed in); writes are recorded only when named with `--tool`, so re-recording never repeats a write by itself.
- **Prompt and skill**: `SUGGEST_CONNECTOR` now says to build from the service's OpenAPI or Swagger document with `connector_from_openapi` when it publishes one, else `connector_write`; `connector_write`'s guide opens by pointing at it and describes fixtures; the `connect-a-service` skill looks for the document in step 1 and generates in step 5 (installed once, so existing homes keep their copy). `connector_from_openapi` is in `NOT_IN_SCRIPTS`.
- **Not done**: GraphQL schemas (spec §6.6 mentions them); external `$ref`s; multipart bodies; servers per operation; response schemas (results are returned as the API sends them).
- **Tests**: tern-lua `tests/openapi.rs` (an OpenAPI 3 YAML document: effects, allowlist, header key, rate, typed and `$ref`/`allOf` params, default selection; OAuth 2 to `capabilities.oauth` with only the needed scopes and a plan, and a built-in profile without one; Swagger 2: base URL, body and form params, query key, stated rate, unsupported uploads, relative servers; a 500-operation document listed, paged, filtered, refused whole and generated from a choice, never activated; a generated connector through `connector_test` against a mock server: approval live, recorded with the key, a token field, a cookie and the injected header absent, replayed with no approval and no request, other arguments live, replay-only refused, a write recorded and replayed, replay with no server or secret, an unrecorded request refused, fixtures moved on activation; a query key never on the tape), `openapi.rs` and `fixtures.rs` unit tests (names, rates, Luau literals, YAML bombs, sensitive names, redaction), and `tern/tests/t4c.rs` through the binary (`from-openapi --list`, default reads from a YAML file, `test --record`, offline replay with the server and secret gone, a changed result failing).

## T2 C5 (channel suggestions, TUI channels, Telegram topics)

Phase C5 of docs/channels-design.md §6 (tooling spec §4.2). Three parts, one commit each.

### Channel suggestions

- **`channel_suggest {slug, title?, why, routines?}`** never creates a channel. It records a suggestion (migration 19, `channel_suggestions`: self-contained, so a merge can renumber it) and writes its card: a `needs_you` post by the run's agent, in the conversation the run is in. That uses the same rule as approvals in place (a routine's run: the channel it posts to; else its session's channel; else Home), so outside Home the C4 pointer reaches Home and the counts work as for any needs-you post. It is tainted when the run is. The tool answers at once ("carry on, you don't need to wait"). It is refused for a slug that exists (the agent is told to post there or let the user move things), for a sub-agent (it says so in its report), and for routines it can't find (by id, or by the start of the prompt, which is how a routine is titled).
- **It's in every agent's system layer** (`agents::SYSTEM_TOOLS`), contained under taint (`TAINT_CONTAINED`: it only writes a card the user decides), and not callable from scripts.
- **A post carries its suggestion** (`Post.suggestion`: slug, title, why, the routines with titles, state, the created channel, the conversation it came from), filled the same way for the card and its Home pointer (`channels::suggestion_of`), live too.
- **Accepting** (`POST /v1/suggestions/{id}/accept {slug?, title?, move_conversation? = true, routines?}`) does several things. It creates the topic channel and adds the suggesting agent as a member. It moves the conversation the suggestion came from with the move feature: if that refuses, for example because the conversation is still working, the channel is still created and `not_moved` says why. It points the chosen routines at the channel (their agents join, AP2). Finally it rewrites the card to what happened ("Created #finances, and moved this conversation there; 1 routine now posts there") and resolves it with its pointer. **Declining** (`…/decline`, or dismissing the card with `POST /v1/posts/{id}/resolve`) rewrites it to "Declined #slug" and resolves it. A suggestion is decided once; a second decision is 409.
- **Desktop**: the card, in a stream (`PostCard`) or a topic timeline, has **Create #slug** and **Not now**, with "Move this conversation there" and one "Move the routine …" checkbox per suggested routine, all on by default. Accepting opens the new channel. A decided card shows **Go to #slug** or "Declined".
- **Tests**: `suggestions_repo::tests`, `tests/c5.rs` (a card in Home and nothing created; accepting creates the channel and moves the conversation and the routine, the card closes and can't be decided again; an existing slug refused to the agent; dismissing declines), `test/suggestion_test.dart` (parsing; accepting with a routine left out; Not now; a decided card).

### TUI channels

- **`/channels`** lists Home and the topic channels with what's working and what needs you (agents' DMs stay `/agent`'s). **`/channel <slug> [message]`** switches the composer to a topic channel: its recent posts are printed, sends go through the channel (`POST /v1/channels/{id}/posts`, so the lead answers, `@mentions` wake agents, and a busy run gets the text queued), and the run that starts is driven and streamed as before. After each run, what else was said there (other agents, routines) is printed, but not the user's own posts or the driven run's answer, which streamed. **`/channel home`** goes back to Home (the TUI's own sessions), and `/agent` leaves a channel, as switching one leaves `/agent`. **`/channel new <slug> [title]`** makes one and switches to it. **`/channel accept|decline <slug>`** answers a channel suggestion shown in Home or the current channel, which print with those two commands.
- **The status line** starts with `#slug ·` while in a channel and ends with `· N need you` (Home's count, which covers everything waiting on the user), refreshed after runs and channel commands.
- **Backends**: attached to the daemon, all of it works over the API. Embedded, without the daemon, channels can be listed, made and read, but posting into one (and deciding a suggestion) says to start the background agent, like the outbox: sending needs the daemon's mention wakes and answer posts.
- **Tests**: `app::tests::channel_commands_switch_post_and_show_what_needs_you` (listing with counts, switching with recent posts and the status line, a send through the channel and the posts printed after, accepting a suggestion, a new channel, Home again, `/agent` leaving).

### Telegram channels (topics, or `#slug ·` prefixes)

- **Bot API versions, checked** (the spec's open question): private chats between a user and a bot got topics in **Bot API 9.3 (31 Dec 2025)**: `User.has_topics_enabled` (the bot's topic mode for private chats, turned on for the bot by its owner) and `message_thread_id` / `is_topic_message` on messages there. Bots could **create** topics in private chats from **Bot API 9.4 (9 Feb 2026)** (`createForumTopic`). Forum supergroups have had topics since 6.3. tern doesn't check versions: it asks for a topic and falls back to prefixes when Telegram refuses.
- **The binding is in the store** (`tern_gateway::chat_channels`, kv `chat_channel` from `telegram:<chat or chat/topic>` to the channel id, and `chat_channel_topic` from `telegram:<chat>:<channel>` to its topic), like AP1's chat → agent binding, so the gateway and the daemon read the same thing.
- **`/channel <name>`**: where the chat has topics (a forum group, or a private chat with topics), the channel gets **one topic, named `#slug`**, made once and reused: a second `/channel` points at the existing topic. Everything written in that topic posts in the channel. Otherwise (`createForumTopic` refused), **the chat follows the channel**: its messages post there, and replies and forwarded posts start `#slug ·`. **`/channel home`** goes back, **`/channel new <name> [title]`** makes one, and **`/channels`** lists them with what needs you. Topics in private chats are recognised by `is_topic_message`, like a forum's.
- **Flat, as everywhere**: a chat's message is the same send as the app's (`Manager::send_to_topic`, with the lead or `@mentions` answering). There are no threads per chat: tern answers a topic and the app in its one conversation in the channel. The first answer is delivered to the chat that wrote, as its reply: `deliver_to` is set on that run before it starts, so a fast run can't finish first. **Other posts in the channel** reach every chat or topic following it, as the gateway reads them from the daemon's change stream (`ChatHost::changes`): another agent's answer as `Research · …`, a routine's result, a needs-you post marked "(needs you)". The user's own posts, notes, pointers, and an answer delivered to that chat already aren't repeated. Replying in a topic continues the channel, because every message there does; a channel has no `/new` (it says so).
- **Agent profiles stay coherent**: a chat follows one channel or one agent at a time. `/agent` in a chat following a channel stops following it. In a channel's own topic, `/agent` is refused (use it in the main chat). Telegram's DM-per-agent topics (AP1) and channel topics coexist in one forum.
- **Open question 9** (one Home across the desktop and Telegram) is untouched: the user's call.
- **Tests**: `tests/c5.rs` `telegram_maps_channels_to_topics_or_prefixes` against the fake Bot API, which now refuses `createForumTopic` in chats without topics and marks private-chat topic messages. It covers a private chat without topics following #finances with prefixes; the send going through the channel; a post from the app reaching the chat by name, with nothing repeated; `/new` refused; Home again; a forum group's one topic, reused; and a private chat with topics getting its own, its answer delivered there and forwarded to the forum's topic. `tests/ap1.rs` still passes.

## T6b (the activity ledger, the Tasks view, `tern security audit`)

Tooling spec §8.1, §8.2, §8.4; wargame W2, W6, W7. Migration `0020_ledger.sql` (renumbered from 19 at merge, after channel suggestions) is self-contained.

### The ledger

- **Its own table, not a view over the journal.** The spec calls it "a view over the journal", but grants, outbox decisions, connections, webhook secrets, devices and settings aren't journal steps, and a view can't be hash-chained once. `ledger` rows: `seq, at, kind, actor, subject, summary, detail (JSON), run_id, session_id, channel_id, routine_id, connector, prev_hash, hash`.
- **The chain**: `hash = sha256(prev_hash || canonical JSON of [seq, at, kind, actor, subject, summary, detail, run_id, session_id, channel_id, routine_id, connector])`, lowercase hex; the first `prev_hash` is 64 zeros. `seq` is in the hash, so reordering shows. `Store::ledger_verify` walks it in order and reports the first entry whose `seq` skips (a deleted row), whose `prev_hash` doesn't link, or whose contents don't match. Cutting entries off the end can't be seen from the chain itself, so `verify` prints the head hash to note elsewhere (an external anchor is future work). Triggers refuse `UPDATE`/`DELETE` as defence in depth; the chain is what detects someone who drops them.
- **One chain, two writers.** The daemon and a CLI command (`tern grants remove`) can both append. `prev_hash` is `UNIQUE`, so a race fails the insert instead of forking the chain, and the append re-reads the head and retries (up to 5 times).
- **Appends happen where the change is made**, on the same connection (inside the same transaction where there is one), so no caller can skip them: `add_allowlist`/`remove_allowlist`/`remove_allowlist_matching` (`grant.add`, `grant.remove`); `approve_outbox`, `reject_outbox`, `expire_outbox` (`outbox.approve|reject|expire`); `add_connection`, `set_default_connection`, `remove_connection` (`connection.*`); `set_webhook_secret` (`webhook.create` or `webhook.rotate`), `revoke_webhook`; `create_device` (`device.pair`, with its scope), `revoke_device`; a routine's autonomy through `create_schedule`, `update_schedule` and `set_schedule_autonomy` when it changes (`autonomy.routine`); an agent's `egress` and `tools` (`egress.agent`, `agent.tools`).
- **The agent loop adds** (`tern-core/src/agent/ledger.rs`): a person's approval decision (`approval`, with `decision` once/always/run/site/denied/timed out, the effect, the pattern an "always" added, the taint), a forbidden call refused (`approval`, actor `tern`), a call queued in the outbox (`outbox.queue`, instead of an "unanswered" decision), and every `write`/`destructive` call that ran (`effect`, actor `agent:<id>`; `connector.activate` for `connector_activate`), from a run or an outbox execution. Host-granted writes (workspace files, memory) are included, since the spec says every write. Reads aren't. Replayed steps add nothing; a crash between a step completing and its entry being written loses that one entry (the journal still has the step).
- **Settings in `config.toml`** (`autonomy`, `[taint] egress`, `[sandbox] network`) aren't changed through tern, so `ledger_note_setting` records them when tern reads the file (`Runtime::open`, and each `set_live`) and they differ from the last value recorded: a hand edit shows up at the next start or reload.
- **Actor**: `owner` for every store-level change, since the store doesn't know which client made it; `tern` for the host's own (expiry, refusals, queueing); `agent:<id>` for calls that ran. A device or member actor is the multiplayer hook (§15.4): the column is there.
- **Channel and routine** are filled from the run's session on append (`sessions.channel_id`, the schedule with that session), so `--channel` and `--routine` filters work without joins and survive the session's deletion.
- **Summaries go through the journal's redaction**, as approval cards do. Webhook secrets and tokens are never passed in; a test checks a rotated secret isn't in any entry.
- **Bundles** (T4b-iii): `Store::ledger_append(&LedgerEntry::new("bundle.install", name, …).connector(name))` is the call to make.
- **CLI, API, app**: `tern ledger [--kind --channel --routine --connector --run --limit --json]` and `tern ledger verify [--json]` open the database directly (work with tern stopped). `GET /v1/ledger`, `GET /v1/ledger/verify` are owner-only (`OWNER_ONLY`), feature `ledger`. The desktop's Library → Activity ledger shows the verdict banner, filter chips by family, "Older" paging, and rows that open their conversation or routine.

### The Tasks view

- **A query over the existing sources** (`daemon/api_tasks.rs::collect`), in five sections: `needs_you` (pending approvals, pending outbox items, secret requests, sign-in cards, from the same lists their endpoints serve), `running` (`Manager::active_runs`, sorted by depth so sub-agents follow their parent and carry `parent_run_id`), `queued` (runs with status `queued`, watch events `pending`/`batch` without a run, held deliveries), `scheduled` (enabled routines: the next time for cron, the interval for watches, "on each delivery" for webhooks) and `failed` (top-level runs failed in the last 24 h, routines paused by a cap). Goals (§10) join `needs_you` when they exist.
- **Each item says where it lives**: `session_id` (the top of a sub-agent's tree for requests), `channel_id`, `agent_id`, `routine_id`. The app opens a request's conversation (where its card is, since approvals-in-place), a run as a thread, and routine items on the Routines page.
- **Read scope**, like the other GETs. The app polls it every 4 s while the page is open rather than mirroring events; the sidebar count reuses what the app already has (open cards in every transcript plus pending outbox), so it needs no poll.
- `tern tasks [--json]` asks the daemon, which alone knows what's running and what waits.

### `tern security audit`

- **`security_cmd::audit(home, config, store)`** is shared by the CLI (config from disk), `GET /v1/security/audit` (the live config, on a blocking thread since it loads connectors) and `doctor` (its counts, plus each high finding in red; `Warn` for highs, `Info` for mediums only, so doctor's exit code is unchanged). Severities `high`, `medium`, `low`, `info`; non-zero exit on any `high`.
- **Graded**: high for `yolo` (global or a routine's, and a watch or webhook routine under it), `remote_mcp_stdio` with a TCP API, a world-readable `secrets.toml`, `[sandbox] network = true` with no OS sandbox, webhooks on a network address without TLS; medium for `autonomous`, legacy `ctx.secret` connectors, no OS sandbox or `confine = "off"`, the host sandbox behind a TCP API, `egress = "allow"` (global or per agent), broad OAuth scopes; low for a network-facing API or webhook listener, owner-scope or 90-day-idle devices, gateways with `strangers = "taint"`, `*` grants, network on with a sandbox. Listeners on loopback, read/chat devices, and tainted-capable routines under a normal autonomy are `info`, listed so the report says what is exposed.
- **"Stale OAuth scopes" became "broad scopes"**: tern doesn't track which scopes a connector's tools use, so it flags well-known broad ones (`https://mail.google.com/`, full Drive, `repo`, `admin:*`, `*.All`, `*`) from the connection's stored `auth.scopes`.
- **Connectors are inspected without secrets**: a `secrets.toml` the loader refuses (its own high finding) would otherwise hide the legacy-connector findings.
- **Bundles** (wired in when T4b-iii merged): `audit()` lists unsigned bundles (medium) and ones signed by a key you haven't trusted (low), from `tern_bundle::installed`.
- **Desktop**: Settings › Security shows the counts and opens the report (info findings folded away until asked).

- **Tests**: `ledger_repo` (verifies; an edit, an edited detail, a deleted row and a rehashed row each caught at the right entry; triggers refuse edits; channel/routine filled and filters; settings noted only on change; two handles on one file extend one chain; every store-level change recorded, no secret in any entry); `tasks_repo`; agent tests (decisions, grants and writes recorded, not reads or replays; denials and refusals; an outbox item from queue to execution); `tests/t6b.rs` (API and CLI with a tamper caught by `tern ledger verify`; Tasks collecting all nine kinds of item plus `tern tasks`; the audit reporting every condition above with its severity, the human form, doctor's section, and the API); `tasks_cmd` and `security_cmd` units; desktop `ledger_test`, `tasks_test`, `security_test`, and `t6b_screenshot_test` (Tasks, the ledger intact and tampered, the audit dialog, light and dark).
- **Note**: `s1::spawns_beyond_the_concurrency_limit_queue_and_still_finish` failed once under a full parallel run and passed four times alone; it depends on a child still running when the next spawn happens, and every write now adds a ledger row.

## T4b-iii (signed bundles, `tern bundle`, `import openclaw`)

Tooling spec §6.5, wargame W2, decision 5 (no public registry). A new crate, `tern-bundle` (`source`, `sign`, `manifest`, `plan`, `lookalike`, `install`, `export`, `openclaw`, `state`), the `tern bundle` and `tern import openclaw` commands, `/v1/bundles`, and Library → Bundles in the app. No migrations: state is files under `~/.tern/bundles/`. One new config section, `[bundles]`.

- **Format.** `bundle.toml` (`[bundle] name, version, description, author, key` and `[[file]] path, sha256`) plus the files, as a directory or a `.tar.zst`. The layout is fixed: `connectors/<name>.luau`, `skills/<slug>/…`, `routines.toml`, `README.md`; anything else, hidden files, `..`, links, devices, or a file the manifest doesn't list is refused (an unlisted file would be covered by neither hash nor signature). Caps: 256 files, 1 MB each, 8 MB in all, 8 MB downloaded. `.tar.zst` is pure Rust (`tar` without default features, `ruzstd`, which also compresses), unpacked in memory, never to disk before it is checked.
- **Signing is minisign's format**, as the spec says: public keys are `RW…` base64, and signatures may be prehashed (`ED`, BLAKE2b-512, minisign's default, which tern writes) or legacy (`Ed`). Checked both ways against rsign2: a key and signature it made are a unit test, and it verified a tern signature. tern's own secret key isn't minisign's (that one is scrypt-encrypted, which would mean a passphrase prompt): a PKCS#8 file, 0600, like `secrets.toml`. `blake2` (RustCrypto, pure Rust, on the `digest` already in the tree) is the one new crypto dependency; ed25519 and SHA-256 are aws-lc-rs.
- **Order of checks**: the pinned hash, then every file's SHA-256 against the manifest, then the signature. A bad signature, a changed file, a pin mismatch, or a signature with no `key` to check it is an error and never a warning. A missing signature (with or without `key`) is "unsigned", which installs with a warning unless `[bundles] require_signed`.
- **The pin** is the SHA-256 of the archive as fetched (what `sha256sum` and a publisher print), or of `bundle.toml` for a directory, which pins every file through the manifest. Given as `--sha256` or `…#sha256=<hex>`; without one, the plan shows the hash and the lockfile records it (trust on first use, said in the plan). `export` prints the hash to share.
- **Trust.** `bundles/trusted.toml` holds keys the user trusts (`tern bundle trust`, `keygen` adds your own). A valid signature by any other key installs with "a key you haven't trusted". The lockfile pins each bundle's key: an update signed by a different key, or unsigned after being signed, is refused without `--accept-new-key` (the app's "I know why the key changed"). No key server, no registry.
- **Fetching** follows the 2afdb49 rules and T4b-i's hop loop: https only, every address of the host public (the client is pinned to the addresses checked), redirects followed by hand (at most 5) with each hop checked again, a size cap. `[bundles] allow_private_hosts` lets a LAN server (http too) through, like `[auth] local_issuers`.
- **The plan** is one struct (`plan::Plan`, serialized as the API's preview) rendered by the CLI and the app: signature state (`unsigned`, `trusted`, `pinned`, `unknown`, `new_key`); per connector, the hosts and paths, `auth` as `Auth::describe` (OAuth entries are the `oauth` line, not a secret sent), OAuth scopes, rate, each tool's effect and flags, legacy `ctx.secret`; per skill, its files and the ones that could be run (by extension or `#!`); per routine, trigger, initiative, autonomy, limits, prompt; the secrets and accounts needed (names and whether set, T4a: requirements, never tokens); warnings. Connectors are loaded in a throwaway VM on a scratch store to read their declarations, as `connector_write` does.
- **Lookalike hosts (W2)** are flagged per connector and in the warnings, in red in the app: punycode or non-ASCII names, bare IPs, a host whose site is within edit distance 1–2 of a known service (about 30 names) or carries its name (`notion-api.co`, `github.com.evil.io`, after undoing digit swaps like `n0tion`), a connector named after a service reaching a site that isn't that service's, and two sites of one connector a letter or two apart. Known sites match by suffix, not registrable domain: the public suffix list's private section makes `googleapis.com` a suffix, which first flagged `gmail.googleapis.com`. It is a heuristic and errs toward speaking up; the allowlist is still what bounds where a connector can send anything.
- **Diff and re-asking.** An update's plan is compared with the plan of the approved copy (`bundles/installed/<name>/`) as sets of capability lines (`connector notion: reaches files.notion.so`, `…: tool x (write)`, `routine r: cron …`, initiative, autonomy, runnable skill files): added, removed, and changed content (prompts, limits, skill files). It asks again when anything was added or the key changed; a shrinking update goes ahead. Unchanged routines keep their id and on/off state; changed ones are recreated paused.
- **What installs, and how.** Connectors are written to `connectors/drafts/` and activated one by one through the existing activation approval (which shows the capability summary again): the bundle approval doesn't stand in for it, so one approval can't switch on a connector's network access by surprise. Routines are created through `tern_tools::schedule::create_routine` and then paused; watches aren't checked at install (their connector is a draft), only their syntax, and a watch on a connector neither bundled nor installed is noted. Skills are installed live: tern has no skill drafts, and the plan showed them. A connector or skill of the user's own (or another bundle's) with the same name is a conflict that stops the install; nothing of the user's is overwritten. `remove` deletes a connector only while it is byte-for-byte the bundle's.
- **Routines in bundles carry no `agent_id`** (T1b's question): an id is local to one tern. The installer picks the agent (`--agent`, default tern, kept on update). No webhook triggers: their secret is made where they run. The spec's `channel` and `model` keys are accepted and shown as not applied (results go to the agent's usual place; per-routine models aren't built). Unknown keys are errors.
- **Agent profile templates (AP3)** were reserved here as `agents/` (refused with "can't be installed by this version of tern yet") and are built now: see "Agent profiles AP3".
- **Export** takes named connectors, skills and routines, or `--all` (active connectors, skills, and one agent's routines, minus tern's unedited built-in skills and anything from a bundle). A draft is exported when there's no active connector of that name, with a note. Routines become templates without agent, channel, delivery target or session, named from their prompt's first words; webhook routines are left out. Hidden files, links, credential-looking names (`*.pem`, `*.key`, `*secret*`, `*credential*`, `id_rsa*`…) in skills stay behind, and the export is refused, naming the file and the secret (never the value), if any file contains the value of a secret in `secrets.toml` (six characters or more).
- **`import openclaw`** (OpenClaw as documented at docs.openclaw.ai on 2026-11-04): the state dir (`openclaw.json` in JSON5, through a small converter for comments, trailing commas, unquoted keys and single quotes) or a workspace. Skills and automations become the unsigned bundle `openclaw-import`, through the same plan and approval, so a re-import is an update with a diff. Current OpenClaw keeps automations in SQLite; tern reads the pre-2026.9.7 `cron/jobs.json` and job JSON saved from `openclaw automations get` as `automations.json`, rather than its database, whose schema isn't documented. Cron stays cron (a `tz` is noted: tern runs local time); `every` becomes cron only when it fits a clock (a day becomes 09:00, noted); future `at` becomes once; `stream` is reported. Jobs that delivered to a chat become `interrupt`, others the `digest` default. SOUL.md + IDENTITY.md replace a default persona, else go beside it as `persona.openclaw.md`; USER.md and MEMORY.md replace defaults or are appended under a heading; none is written twice. The spec said skills come in as drafts; tern has no skill drafts, so they install live after the plan is approved.
- **Credentials never cross.** Every string under a credential-like key in `openclaw.json` is collected only to refuse any skill file containing one; `credentials/` is never opened. The report lists secret names (skills' `requires.env`, `skills.entries.*.env` and `apiKey` via `primaryEnv`), channels to set up (Telegram's config and pairing, `tern gateway add` for Slack and ntfy), MCP servers with the `tern mcp add` line, providers (`tern setup`), and what isn't converted (plugins, heartbeat, AGENTS.md and the like, daily logs, other agents).
- **For T6b**: `tern_bundle::installed(home)` is every installed bundle with `key`, `key_trusted`, source, hash and contents, and `tern_bundle::unsigned(home)` the ones `tern security audit` should list. Both only read the lockfile and never fail. Installs and removals are logged with `tracing` (target `tern_bundle::install`, fields bundle, version, sha256, source); the ledger can record them from `Installer::install`/`remove`.
- **API and app**: `/v1/bundles` is owner-only (installing adds code and routines, and fetching makes the core reach a URL) and advertised as the `bundles` feature. `POST /v1/bundles/install` requires the preview's `sha256`, so the app installs exactly the bytes it showed. The app's install dialog asks for a link or path, shows the plan, and says what to do next (activate connectors, turn routines on, set secrets, add accounts).
- **Not done**: activating connectors from the app (it goes through the agent, as before), a curated index, `git` sources (clone, then install the directory), signing with a minisign secret key file, the TUI, and per-file diffs of skill text.
- **Tests**: `tern-bundle` unit tests (signatures valid, invalid, missing, legacy and an rsign2 vector; hash mismatch and changed files; unlisted files, links in directories and archives; deterministic packing; fetch refusing http and private addresses and checking redirects; paths; lookalikes; JSON5; intervals); `tests/install.rs` (the plan's contents, the W2 lookalike, install as drafts and paused routines, the lockfile, a shrinking update that keeps a routine on, a growing one's diff, key change and signed-to-unsigned refused, remove, your own connector not overwritten, pins); `tests/export.rs` (no secret value, delivery target or session in any file, hidden and credential files left out, webhook routines left out, a secret value in a skill refusing the export, an export installing elsewhere); `tests/openclaw.rs` against `tests/fixtures/openclaw` (skills, routines, the leaky skill refused, every fake credential absent, setup lines, profile files written once, a bare workspace keeping your persona); `crates/tern/tests/bundles.rs` against the daemon (preview, install pinned to the preview, a file added after the preview refused, remove); `tern_client` and desktop tests from real API output, and `bundles_screenshot_test.dart`, looked at by eye.

## Starter agents catalog

- **Starters are data.** `crates/tern-core/assets/starters.toml` holds every starter (`[[starter]]`: id, name, one-line description, category, persona, memory, avatar, optional routine, optional `[starter.tools]`), embedded as `defaults::STARTERS_TOML` and parsed once by `agents::templates()`. `Template` now owns its strings and gained `category`; the API, `Runtime::create_agent` and the create sheet were not otherwise touched. The four originals (research, inbox, marketing, blank) keep their ids and text; Inbox's scope was tightened (no shell, no writes, no MCP; connectors and the schedule tools stay).
- **One validator.** `Template::validate` runs on every starter at parse time: the slug a user-created agent would get, memory, a tool scope that parses as `ToolScope::parse` does, a real cron for the routine, avatar from the picker's glyphs and colours, a persona with the "- Your job:" line delegation reads (Blank is exempt), a one-line description. Catalog rules on top: built-ins are listed (never "all"), only names in `STARTER_TOOLS`, no MCP "all", and `shell` only for Coding. A bad catalog logs an error and serves no starters rather than panicking. `tests/starters.rs` creates every starter through `POST /v1/agents` against the real registry's tool list.
- **Power follows the job.** Coding starters get file tools and `shell`, with no connectors or MCP; the reviewer, security reviewer and PR summarizer get `shell` for read-only git but no write or edit tool. Docs Writer gets files but no shell. Everyone else is files-and-web at most. Roles that act through services (mail, calendar, tickets, CRM, accounting) get `connectors = "all"` because connector names are the user's, and their personas say never to send, pay, refund or change records without a go-ahead. Approvals are still the global gate, and each starter now also carries a ceiling ("Starter autonomy ceilings" below). Memory is private for every starter.
- **UIs.** The desktop picker groups chips by category (first appearance in the file) and adds a search box once there are more than eight starters (name, description, category, id; the chosen starter stays visible). `tern agents templates` prints the catalog by category. The TUI has no template picker.
- **Tests**: `agents::tests` (catalog parses and validates, unique names, contiguous categories, least power, rejection of bad starters), `tests/starters.rs`, and the Flutter picker test plus the client's `category` parsing.

### Starter autonomy ceilings

- **Optional `autonomy` on a starter** (`readonly` or `supervised`, the core's level names), carried by `Template` and served by `GET /v1/agents/templates`. `Runtime::create_agent` uses it when the request has no `autonomy` (`a.autonomy.or(template.autonomy)`), so a level the user chose always wins. It is a ceiling, as in AP3: `min(configured, ceiling)`. Every shipped starter sets one, with the reason in a comment above it in `starters.toml`.
- **By risk.** `readonly` (it can only look; every non-read call is refused): research, deep-research, fact-checker, and meeting-prep (read-only built-ins; its connector reads work, any connector write is refused, and it only drafts follow-ups as text). `supervised` for everything else: the connector agents (inbox, assistant, project-manager, social-media, customer-support, sales-outreach, bookkeeper), the coding, writing and data agents (bug-fixer, test-writer, refactorer, devops, docs-writer, writer-editor, marketing, tutor, data-analyst), competitive-intel (schedule tools) and Blank. The reviewers (code-reviewer, security-reviewer, pr-summarizer) are `supervised`, not `readonly`: `shell` is never a read effect, so a readonly ceiling would refuse their read-only git commands outright. Tutor and competitive-intel were not made readonly because they write notes and schedules; dropping those tools to fit the ceiling would change what the starters do.
- **Cost of `readonly`: no memory writes.** `memory_save` is a write effect and not in `TAINT_CONTAINED`, so a readonly agent (research, deep-research, fact-checker, meeting-prep) cannot save memories, though it can search them. That follows the ceiling's definition and was left as is; if research agents should learn, either move them to `supervised` or let the memory tools through under `readonly` in `approval::check` (a core policy change, not made here).
- **Validation** (`Template::validate`): the level must parse (an unknown name fails the whole catalog's parse), may not exceed `supervised` (`STARTER_MAX_AUTONOMY`), and a `readonly` starter may not have `write_file`, `edit_file`, `shell` or the schedule write tools, nor leave `tools` out (the narrow default includes file writes). A unit test requires every starter to have a ceiling, and `tests/starters.rs` checks the created agent's ceiling (and the served template's) equals the starter's, and that an explicit `autonomy` in the request beats it.
- **UIs.** `tern agents templates` prints the ceiling in its own column. The desktop picker's tooltip adds "<Level> ceiling" and a line under the chips says what the chosen starter's ceiling means; choosing a starter pre-selects it in the sheet's autonomy picker, which the user can still change before Create.

## T5b (skill capture)

Stage 3 of docs/personal-agent-tooling-spec.md §7. Pure logic is `tern-core/src/skill_capture.rs` and the draft helpers in `skills.rs`; the daemon side is `daemon/api_skills.rs` (also the offer and accept code, on `Manager`); the table is migration 21 (`skill_offers`).

- **The host makes the offer, not the model.** The spec says "the agent may offer once"; a tool the model calls would add a tool to every prompt and rely on its judgment. The daemon decides from the run itself, once the run has finished and its post is written (`Manager::spawn`, next to `post_run_result`): no model call, no prompt change.
- **What qualifies** (`maybe_offer_skill`): top-level, `completed`, not `quiet`, trigger not `cron`/`outbox`/`mcp`, never tainted (`taint` and `input_taint` both null), at least `[skills].capture_min_tool_calls` tool calls (default 8, counted from the run's `tool_use` blocks) and no more than a quarter of them errors. **"Marked good or didn't correct" is approximated**: there is no thumbs-up, and the next user message isn't known when the run ends, so tool errors (a denied approval is an error result) stand in for "got corrected", and accepting is the "marked good". A later message that corrects the run doesn't withdraw the offer; the user reads the draft anyway.
- **Tainted runs are never offered.** A run that read a web page or an email could carry that text's instructions into a skill that every later run loads. Untrusted content in a run means no offer at all, which is stricter than the spec; the drafting prompt also says not to copy instructions out of tool results.
- **Once**: `skill_offers.run_id` is unique, so a run is offered once whatever came of it; and a conversation with an offer pending isn't offered again (`create_skill_offer` returns `None`), so a long chat doesn't ask after every message. A decided offer frees the conversation for a later run. Pending offers lapse after 7 days (`OFFER_TTL_MS`): they drop out of the list and Tasks, and accepting one answers 410. Decided offers stay as history.
- **Where the offer shows**: `GET /v1/skill-offers` (default pending), a `skill_offer` row in Tasks under "needs you" (so the desktop Tasks view and its badge pick it up with no app change), `tern skills offers`, `/skills offers` in the TUI. No post and no event: Home's conversation isn't posts, and `Change` is matched by the desktop client, so a new variant would need the app to change with it. Clients re-read on the Tasks refresh.
- **Accepting** (`Manager::capture_skill`) makes one model call with the run's own model, `DRAFT_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, no tools, 2500 tokens, 3 minutes, over `compaction::transcript` of the run's messages (the user's input, every assistant turn, tool calls and results; tool results clipped to 4 KB each, the whole to 60 KB in the middle). The call isn't journaled and isn't counted against a budget (there is no run to hang it on). A failed call leaves the offer pending, so it can be retried.
- **The draft** (`finish_draft`): the reply must have `name` and `description` frontmatter and a non-empty body (a code fence round it is stripped). It is rewritten so `name` is the slug (lowercase, hyphens, 48 characters; `--name` overrides), the description is one line, unknown keys are dropped, and the frontmatter gets `draft: true` and `captured_from: <run id>`. It is written to `skills/<slug>/SKILL.md`; if that name is taken (by a directory or another skill's `name`) it becomes `<slug>-2`, so a capture never overwrites anything. The offer then records the draft's name. A second accept racing the first writes a draft and discards it again (`decide_skill_offer` is the arbiter).
- **Drafts are inert until published.** `skills::discover` (the prompt's index, `skill_read`, bundle export) skips `draft: true`; `discover_all` returns both with `Skill::draft`. `publish_draft` removes the `draft:` line and nothing else; `discard_draft` deletes the directory. Both refuse a published skill. The agent has no way to publish: it is `tern skills publish`, `/skills publish` or `POST /v1/skills/{name}/publish`, all the user's. A user who deletes `draft: true` by hand has published it.
- **API**: `/v1/skills` and `/v1/skill-offers` (spec in the `api_skills.rs` header). Reads are `read` scope, as everything GET; the writes are owner-only because no `CHAT_WRITES` entry lists them. `/v1/health` features gains `skill_capture`.
- **CLI and TUI**: `tern skills [list|show|publish|discard]` work on the folder directly (no daemon needed); `offers`, `save`, `dismiss` need the daemon, since it watched the runs and holds the model. TUI `/skills [offers|save N [name]|dismiss N|show|publish|discard NAME]` goes through a new `Backend::skills(args)` that only the remote backend implements; the in-process TUI says to start the daemon, as `/outbox` does for decisions.
- **Offers only come from the daemon.** `tern run` and the in-process TUI don't use `Manager::spawn`, so their runs aren't offered. Telegram and desktop runs are (they go through the manager).
- **Mock provider**: answers draft requests with the script's `skill_draft` (a SKILL.md) or a fixed one.
- **Tests**: unit tests in `skill_capture.rs` (shape, threshold, slugs, rewriting, rejection), `skills.rs` (draft listing, naming clashes, publish and discard) and `skill_offers_repo.rs` (once per run, once per pending conversation, lapse); a TUI test for `/skills`; end to end in `tests/t5b.rs` (offer after a long run, Tasks row, accept writes a draft the next prompt doesn't list, publish makes it appear, 409 on a second decision, dismissal, short runs and `capture = false` make no offer, the default minimum of 8, one offer per conversation).
- **Not done**: a desktop view (the offer shows as a Tasks row, but accepting and reviewing a draft happen in the CLI or TUI; the API for a Skills view is there); `tern doctor` listing drafts; offering from `tern run`; revising a draft with the model; editing `user.md`-style "learned from" provenance beyond `captured_from`; a `Change` event for new offers; counting the drafting call against a budget.

## Agent profiles AP3 (autonomy ceiling, templates as bundles, "Let agents decide", a bot per agent)

docs/agent-profiles-design.md §8, built with the recommended answers in §9 (items 2 to 4 there stay as recommended; nothing in §9 changed). Delegation to profiles was already built ("Agent profiles: delegation"). Migrations 0022 (`agents.autonomy`) and 0023 (`channels.let_agents_decide`); `agents.telegram` was already in 0013.

- **Autonomy ceiling.** `agents.autonomy` (`readonly`, `supervised`, `autonomous`, `yolo`; NULL = no ceiling) is a ceiling, never a grant: a run's frozen autonomy is `min(requested, ceiling)` where `requested` is the configured `autonomy` (`Runtime::start_run`) or a routine's own (`drive_routine_run`), through one function, `Runtime::autonomy_for`. A delegated child (`agent_spawn {agent}`) takes the lower of its caller's autonomy and its own agent's ceiling. Set on `POST`/`PATCH /v1/agents` (`autonomy`; `null` removes it), `tern agents new --autonomy` and `tern agents autonomy <agent> <level|none>`; each change is a ledger entry (`autonomy.agent`). Bundles may carry one (below). Side fix: `Autonomy::ReadOnly` serialized as `read_only` under `rename_all = "snake_case"`, so `autonomy = "readonly"` in `config.toml` and a JSON body were rejected; it is `readonly` now (`read_only` still accepted).
- **Templates as bundles.** The layout's reserved `agents/` is now `agents/<slug>/agent.toml` + `agents/<slug>/persona.md` (both required, checked when the manifest is read). `agent.toml` (`manifest::AgentTemplate`): `name`, `description`, `avatar {glyph, color}`, `memory` (default `private`), `tools` (a `ToolScope`; absent = read-only built-ins, no connectors, no MCP, all skills, so it fails closed), `autonomy` (a ceiling). Unknown keys are errors, so a model, `egress`, `delegates` or a Telegram token can't ride along: nothing machine-specific or security-widening travels, and the user sets those after installing. The plan lists each agent (memory, one line per tool kind, ceiling, the start of the persona), warns when it can use every built-in tool (shell included), every connector or MCP server (including later ones) or the shared memory, and adds capability lines (`agent @x`, `agent @x: connectors: all`, `…: autonomy ceiling …`) so an update that widens an agent is re-approved like any other growth. Install creates the agent and its DM (persona written atomically, row via `create_agent`) and records it in the lockfile (`agents = [{slug, id}]`). It never writes over an existing agent: a slug you already have is a conflict (`Plan::conflicts`, refused at install too), and an update leaves an agent this bundle made as you have it (said in the plan and the outcome), so your edits to its persona survive. An update that drops an agent leaves it, yours now. `remove` archives the agents it made (never deletes: persona and history stay; reinstalling then conflicts until it is unarchived or renamed) and deletes its routines. A routine template may name `agent = "<slug>"` (a slug in the same bundle; unknown is an error); without it, a routine goes to the bundle's only agent, else to `--agent`/tern as before. `tern bundle export --profile <agent>` packages an agent (never the default agent, its model, memory or bot) with its routines as that agent's; the no-secret check covers the persona too. The app's preview/install dialogs show the new `agents` array of the plan as data they ignore for now. Not atomic: an install that fails after making an agent leaves it, as connectors and skills already were.
- **"Let agents decide".** `channels.let_agents_decide` (off by default; `PATCH /v1/channels/{id}`, topic channels only, shown in the channel's `agents`). On, a fresh post that mentions no one (not a reply) gets one call on the gate model (`routines::gate_model`, the cheapest priced model, or `[schedules] gate_model`; refactored out of `ask_gate` as `gate_text`) with the members' `slug · Name · first line of persona`, the lead first, and answers `{"agents": [...]}`. Only members can be named, at most three, repeats and `@` and case ignored; the post then goes to those agents in their own threads, with a quiet "Sent to @x" note. Any failure (no provider, a 20 s timeout, text that isn't the JSON, nobody valid) means the lead answers, as without the setting; mentions and replies skip it; a channel with one member doesn't call. The call sees only your own post, tool-less, and its answer can only choose among members, so text in a post can't send it anywhere else. It costs one cheap call per unmentioned post, and the note says where it went.
- **A bot per agent.** `agents.telegram` is `{bot_token_secret, api_url?}`: the secret's name, never the token (`tern secrets set`). The daemon runs one `TelegramGateway::for_agent` per active agent that has one, as gateway `telegram-<slug>` (kind `telegram`), at start; a secret that isn't set, or a token shared with the main bot or another agent, is skipped with a warning (`tern doctor` checks each secret; `/v1/agents` and the CLI refuse a shared name up front). Chats on it talk to that agent only (`chat_agents::bind_bot`, kv `bot_agent`, checked first by `chat_agents::get`, archived or not, so an archived agent's bot is refused rather than answered by tern): its sessions are source `telegram-<slug>`, threads of its DM, replies have no name prefix, `/agent` and `/channel` say the bot is that agent's own chat, `/new /model /stop /status /outbox` and approvals work as on the main bot. Pairing is the main bot's: `telegram_pairings` is per Telegram user id, so pairing once on any bot trusts you on all; strangers get the private-bot reply. Changes apply when the daemon restarts (the gateway map is built once, as before). `api_url` is only for tests and self-hosted API servers and **can't be set through the API** (the token would go to whatever host it named); the owner-only API takes `telegram: {bot_token_secret}` or `null`. `tern agents telegram <agent> <secret|none>`, `own bot` in `tern agents`, an info line in `tern security audit`, ledger `telegram.agent`. No forum-topic mode on agent bots (each is already its own contact), and no `/channel` from them.
- **Tests**: `tests/ap3.rs` (the ceiling on DM runs, routines and delegated children, never raised, ledgered; routing to one, several, a bad answer, a non-member, mentions beating it, off meaning the lead; a bot per agent against two fake Bot APIs with shared pairing, a stranger, no prefix, the session's source and DM, `/agent` refused, the main bot unchanged, secret sharing refused through CLI and API, `api_url` refused), `tern-bundle/tests/agents.rs` (the plan, install, lock, routines' owner, an update leaving your edits, remove archiving, conflicts, widening updates re-approved, bad templates, export and install elsewhere without the model), `manifest` and `agents` unit tests (paths, router answers and prompt). `tests/ap2.rs`'s channel JSON gained `let_agents_decide`.
- **Not done**: (the desktop UI is built, see "AP3 in the desktop" below), applying a bot change without a restart, and the T3 outbox note in §8 (delegated writes at night already queue through the outbox).

## Editing and deleting (agents, channels, and what else you create)

Agents and channels could be made and archived, but not renamed past their title, restored, or deleted. Now every surface that creates them can also change and delete them: the API first, then `tern agents` / `tern channels`, the TUI and the desktop app. The rules live once, in `crates/tern/src/manage.rs` (`Runtime::rename_agent`, `agent_impact`, `delete_agent`, `channel_impact`, `delete_channel`, `rename_channel_slug`), so the daemon API, the CLI (which works on the database directly) and the TUI's local backend can't drift apart.

- **Deleting an agent is a tombstone, not a row delete.** Migration 0024 adds `agents.deleted_at`. The row stays, so every session, run, post and ledger entry that names the agent still resolves to its name, and the foreign keys on `sessions.agent_id` and friends hold. A deleted agent is invisible to `store.agent()` (by id or slug) and `store.agents()`; `agent_or_deleted(id)` and `deleted_agents()` (API: `GET /v1/agents?deleted=1`, deleted ones marked `deleted`) are how history names it. Its slug and DM slug are rewritten to `~<id>` (a form no user slug can take), so the name is free again; a new agent with the same name gets the next free id (`tern:research-2`) rather than reusing the old one. Its conversations and posts are untouched; its DM stays, archived and hidden. Continuing one of its old conversations is refused ("Research was deleted; its conversations stay readable but can't be continued").
- **What goes with it**: its private memories (`memories.agent_id`), its membership rows and its name in other agents' `delegates`. Its `~/.tern/agents/<id>/` (persona, private `memory.md`) is moved to `agents/.deleted/<name>-<time>`, not removed, so a deletion loses nothing a user could want back; it's the one place nothing cleans up. The ledger gets an `agent.delete` entry (and `channel.delete`).
- **What refuses it** (409 with the reason; `GET .../impact` lists them as `blockers` so clients can disable the button): the default agent (rename it instead; its `@tern` and Home stay), a run of its in progress (`runs.status = 'running'` in its sessions, which includes work delegated to it), and writes of its waiting in the outbox. **Routines** are the one thing that needs a choice: `reassign_to=<agent>` hands them over (their sessions follow, and results that went to its DM go to the new agent's DM), `delete_routines=true` deletes them (webhooks cascade); with neither, and routines present, it refuses and names them. Topic channels it leads go to the agent taking its routines, else to `tern`. Choosing both is refused.
- **Deleting a topic channel deletes its posts, not its conversations.** Posts are the channel's public timeline, so they go, with Home's pointers to its needs-you posts and `runs.woken_by` links (nulled). Its conversations (`sessions`) move under Home (`channel_id = 'home'`), so History still lists and opens them. Routines that posted there post to their owning agent's DM instead. Member rows go. Refused for Home and DMs ("a DM goes with its agent": delete the agent) and while a run is in progress there. Archiving stays the way to hide a channel and keep everything.
- **Confirmation is explicit on every surface.** API: `DELETE ...?confirm=true` (without it, 400 pointing at `GET .../impact`); CLI: the impact is printed, then you type the slug, or pass `--yes` (no terminal and no `--yes` is an error, as in `tern reset`); TUI: `/agents delete <@name> [to <agent> | routines]` and `/channel delete <slug>` print the impact and the exact command to repeat with `yes`; desktop: a dialog that loads the impact, shows what goes and what stays, makes the routine choice (hand over to an agent, default tern, or delete) and disables Delete while there are blockers.
- **Renaming.** Agents already had `PATCH name/slug` (the id never changes, the DM follows); the CLI (`tern agents rename`), the TUI (`/agents rename|slug`) and the desktop sheet (an `@name` field) now reach it. Channels gain `slug` on `PATCH /v1/channels/{id}` (topic channels only; unique, same form as creation); posts, routines and sessions refer to the channel's id, so nothing else changes. Home and DMs can't be renamed here.
- **Restoring.** Archived agents and channels had no way back in the app: Settings has none, so the sidebar's CHANNELS section ends with "Archived…", a list with Restore and Delete… for each. The CLI has `tern agents archive --undo` and `tern channels archive --undo`.
- **New CLI**: `tern channels` (`list [--all]`, `new`, `edit --slug/--title/--instructions`, `archive [--undo]`, `delete [--yes]`); `tern agents rename`, `tern agents delete [--reassign-to A | --delete-routines] [--yes]`. TUI: `/channel rename|title|delete`, `/agents rename|slug|delete`. Desktop: right-click a sidebar agent or channel for Edit / Delete…; Delete… is also in each edit sheet.
- **Other things you create** were audited and already had the full set, so nothing was added: routines (`PATCH`/`DELETE /v1/schedules/{id}`, including the `agent` and `channel` they move to, in the Routines view), webhooks (create, rotate, revoke), connections (add, rename, remove, default), MCP servers (add, edit, remove), secrets (set, delete), bundles (install, remove), Telegram pairings (create, unpair). The CLI has no routine editor because it has no routine creator (agents and the app make them). Per-agent Telegram bots (AP3, another branch) will need their token secret and poller dropped in `delete_agent`; the hook is `delete_agent` in `crates/tern-store/src/manage_repo.rs`.
- **After AP3 (per-agent Telegram bots, agent templates as bundles)**: deleting an agent with its own bot drops the `agents.telegram` reference (the tombstone has none, so no poller is built for it at the next start; the ledger entry records which secret it named), and the daemon API aborts the running `telegram-<slug>` poller at once (`stop_bot`). The token secret is never deleted: it is the user's named secret; impact and the CLI say it stays (`tern secrets` removes it). The CLI works on the database, so a running daemon keeps polling the bot until its restart; its messages are refused meanwhile, because the bot stays bound to the deleted agent (a run for a deleted agent errors, it is never handed to tern). Bundle-installed agents: `tern_bundle::forget_agent` removes the agent from every bundle's lockfile record when it is deleted, so `bundle remove` doesn't claim to archive an agent that is gone, and updating or installing the bundle again makes a fresh agent (id `tern:<slug>-2`: the old id keeps naming the deleted agent's history; `make_agent` now picks the next free id like `create_agent`). A lockfile that wasn't updated (a delete made some other way) still works: `remove` says "already gone". Impact lists the bot's secret and the bundles. Tests: `edit_delete::deleting_an_agent_with_its_own_bot_names_the_bot_and_keeps_the_secret`, `manage_tests::deleting_an_agent_drops_its_bot_reference_only`, and in `tern-bundle/tests/agents.rs` the forget, reinstall and already-gone cases.
- **Migration number**: 0024 (`0024_agent_tombstones.sql`), self-contained; renumber it if another branch took 0024 first. `AgentRow` gains `deleted_at` and `AGENT_COLS` one column; both are one-line merges.
- **Not done**: editing a post (channels-design open question 6), deleting a single memory from the CLI, undoing a delete (the persona files are kept; the rows are not restorable), and merging two agents.
- **Tests**: `tern-store` `manage_tests` (tombstoning keeps history resolvable, the slug is reusable, delegations and memories cleaned, the ledger entry; deleting routines and the lead guard; channel deletion keeps conversations, nulls links, reroutes routines, refuses Home and DMs; channel slug rename), `tests/edit_delete.rs` (the API flows end to end: confirm required, default agent refused, routines must be placed, a run in progress blocks, reassigning, `deleted=1`, the persona kept, a deleted agent's conversation refused, the id reused as `-2`; channel rename, impact, refusals, delete, routine rerouted, the conversation still readable; and the CLI with and without `--yes`), the TUI's `channels_and_agents_are_renamed_and_deleted_only_after_confirming`, the Dart client's impact models, and `test/manage_test.dart` (both delete dialogs, the routine choice, blockers disabling Delete, the `@name` and `#name` fields, no Delete for the default agent, the Archived list).

## AP3 in the desktop (autonomy ceiling, Let agents decide, an agent's bot, agents in bundles)

The UI for the AP3 API; no Rust changed.

- **Autonomy ceiling** (`AutonomyPicker`, `ui/agent_ap3.dart`): a radio list on the agent sheet (also when creating, and for the default agent): No ceiling, then at most read-only, supervised, autonomous, yolo, each with the core's one-line meaning. The text says it is a cap, not a grant (lower of it and the configured autonomy; sub-agents take the lower of caller and own), and that a yolo ceiling lowers nothing. "No ceiling" sends an explicit `autonomy: null` (an absent key means unchanged); unchanged saves send nothing. An agent with a ceiling shows a "Capped: <level>" chip beside its name in its chat header, with a tooltip.
- **Let agents decide**: a switch under "Agents can wake each other" in the channel dialog (topic channels only, as the dialog's agents block already is), with a two-sentence line for each state; sent only when changed. The "Sent to @x" routing note is an ordinary `note` post, which the channel view already rendered; a test now pins it.
- **An agent's bot** (`TelegramBotField`): a dropdown over the user's secrets by NAME (never a value), "No bot of its own" first. A secret that is the main bot's token (`used_by` contains `telegram`), another agent's bot, or unset is listed but disabled with the reason; "Add the bot token as a secret…" opens the Secrets dialog and reloads. The daemon stays the authority (shared-token refusal, name check): its message shows in the sheet and the sheet stays open. Shown only when editing a non-default agent (create takes no `telegram`; the default agent's bot is `[gateway.telegram]`). Saying "applies when the daemon restarts" is in the field and in a snackbar after a bot change.
- **Bundles**: `BundlePlan.agents` renders one card per agent (name, memory, tool lines, ceiling, persona start), its own warnings from the plan (every built-in tool incl. shell, every connector/MCP server, shared memory) and, on updates, the `agent @x…` lines of `changes.added` as "Added capability". An agent that already exists blocks the install (the plan's conflict, in red on the card); one the bundle made earlier says an update leaves it as you have it. Those warnings are shown on the card and left out of the general WARNINGS list rather than twice. The installed list shows `Agents: @x` and the remove dialog says they are archived, not deleted.
- **Client**: `Agent.autonomy`/`telegramSecret`/`autonomyLevel`, `AutonomyLevel`, `ChannelAgents.letAgentsDecide`, `BundleAgent`, `InstalledBundle.agents`; `createAgent(autonomy:)`, `setAgentAutonomy`, `setAgentTelegram`, `updateChannel(letAgentsDecide:)`.
- **Also fixed**: `ui/items.dart` had a `case final x? ? a : b` expression the current analyzer rejects (main did not analyze); rewritten as a `switch`. The channel dialog scrolls now, as it grew.
- **Tests**: `tern_client` `agents_test`, `bundles_test` (fixture `bundle_preview_agents.json`, hand-written to the `AgentPlan` serialization), `ap3_requests_test` (request bodies over a local HTTPS server); desktop `agents_test` (AP3 group), `channel_agents_test`, `bundles_test`, `ap3_screenshot_test` (PNGs in `build/screenshots/ap3_*`).
- **Not done**: the screens were checked as rendered widgets (screenshot tests with system fonts), not in the running macOS app against a daemon; no live indicator of whether an agent's bot is actually running.

### Slimmer agent sheet (create and edit)

- **Starter picker is one select, not chips.** `StarterField` (`ui/agent_starters.dart`) is a single "Start from" field showing the chosen starter and its ceiling; it opens a dialog with an always-present search box and a list grouped under small category headers. A row is the name, a one-line description and the ceiling at the right. Typing filters and aims at the first match; Up/Down move, Enter picks, Esc closes. Blank is always the first row, so there is a clear way back. Keys kept: `starter-<id>`, `starter-category-<c>`, `starter-search`, `starter-ceiling` (now the field's "Read-only ceiling" text), plus `starter-select`.
- **Common path first.** Visible: starter, name (with @slug as a suffix), persona (4 lines), model, the starter's routine checkbox. Everything else is under a collapsed **Advanced** tile (`advanced-toggle`) whose subtitle summarizes tools, memory, ceiling and bot: avatar, sub-agent model, tools, memory (two columns), browsing, autonomy ceiling, can-hand-work-to, Telegram bot. Nothing was removed; edit uses the same sheet, with Advanced collapsed too. Save errors show above the buttons so a bot-name refusal is visible without opening Advanced.
- **Tests**: widget tests open the select with `pickStarter` and Advanced with `openAdvanced`; `ap3_screenshot_test.dart` renders the create flow in light and dark at 1280x900 and 1100x680.

## Scoped approval grants (stop asking about the same thing)

- **Why `connector_test` re-asked every call.** Testing a draft that reaches the network or reads secrets is `forced` to ask (it is code the model just wrote), is not host-granted, and `allow_always()` is false, so the only grant machinery (shell `pattern_for`, the tainted `host:` grant) could never apply: no pattern, no "always", and `AllowRun` only meant something for the host limit. Every call of the same draft tool was therefore a fresh prompt, with only Allow once / Deny. Not taint, not autonomy: a `supervised`, untainted run asked because nothing could remember the answer. Fixed by giving tools a precise grant key rather than loosening any rule.
- **A grant key (`Tool::grant_key` -> `GrantKey { key, label }`) names exactly what the user saw.** For `connector_test`: draft name and tool, a blake3 digest of the draft's source hash, declared http hosts/paths, secrets, auth, rate, the tool's effect, offline-replay vs live, and the `connection` (account). So a grant never covers a different tool, a different draft, a draft edited since, a new host or secret, a different effect, or another account. Arguments are not in the key: they can only reach what the capabilities allow (the same call-time `verify` still refuses a draft that changed after it was shown). Tools without a key behave as before; shell keeps its command patterns.
- **Three scopes, one rule: grants only act through `approval::check`.** "This run" and "this conversation" are in memory (`Agent.scoped`, keyed on run id or session id, tool, key; a daemon restart forgets them and asks again, the safe way round); "always" is `key:<key>` on the tool's allowlist (`tern grants` lists and removes it). They feed the existing `granted` flag, so everything `check` already does still applies: a tainted run ignores every grant and offers no scope (the request has `grant: None`, so a tainted card is Allow once / Deny exactly as before), a `destructive` effect asks every time with no scope, `readonly` still refuses non-reads, `yolo`/`autonomous` behave as before, forbidden tools stay forbidden, and the ledger still records each effect and each decision (new `allowed for this conversation` label; the grant key is in the decision's detail).
- **API**: `ApiApproval` gains `grant {key, label}` and `count`; `POST /v1/approvals/{id}` accepts `session` (and the existing `run`, `always`). `Decision::AllowSession` is new; `AllowRun` means "this run" when a grant is offered, still "all sites for this run" for the host limit (never both on one request).
- **Coalescing.** Identical simultaneous pending requests (same run, call, input, summary, effect, pattern, grant, taint, host, script) share one card (`ApiApprovals` cards hold several waiters; clients get the same `id` again with a higher `count`, and its waiting post is opened once). One answer answers all. A run/conversation/always answer also answers "once" for the other waiting cards with the same tool and key in the same run / conversation / anywhere, and only for cards that offered a grant, so never a tainted or destructive one. Calls in one turn run one at a time anyway (the next is released by the grant before it asks); this is for scripts' `gather` and parallel runs.
- **Desktop**: the card shows Allow for this run / Allow for this conversation / Always allow this when a grant is offered (count in the title); consecutive identical approved requests, each with its finished one-call tool card, show as one line `connector_test · draft x.y ×8 approved` that expands (`groupApprovals`, applied to the conversation, thread pane and sub-agent views, to live cards and to answered `needs_you` notes). Denials, a call still running, and anything different stay separate. The Tasks needs-you title carries `×N` for a shared card.
- **TUI** `[r]` this run, `[c]` this conversation, `[a]` always (with a grant); `tern run`'s terminal prompt accepts `r`/`c`/`a` too. **Telegram and Lua gateways** keep Allow once / Deny for these (their buttons are built from `pattern` and the host limit only); `Decision::AllowSession` is mapped to a label there.
- **After security review**: (1) `connector_test` builds its grant key in `policy()` from the same snapshot (source hash, capabilities, planned replay-or-live) and `grant_key` only peeks it; `call` refuses if the draft's source differs from the one planned, so a fixture recorded in between can't turn a live call's key into the offline one's. (2) A call released by someone else's scoped answer is `Decision::CoveredByGrant` / outcome `approved_by_grant`: it runs, but the ledger says "covered by a grant" (with the key), the post says "Covered by a grant:", and the transcript line says so; no human "allowed once" is recorded for a call nobody saw. (3) The key's account part is the resolved connection (id, slug, account address), so another account or a changed default asks again; an unresolvable account gets no key. (4) A remote device's answer never releases a `local_only` card. (5) The grant offer tests the run's taint itself (TAINT_CONTAINED tools make `check` ask as if clean). (6) With a grant offered the request carries no `pattern`, and "always" stores the key only.
- **Review round 2**: (a) `connector_test`'s plan (replay-or-live, source hash, grant key, resolved account) is kept per call, by (run id, call id), not per draft+tool+args: the registry tells the tool which call it is planning for (`tern_core::tool::planning_scope`, set around `policy` and `grant_key`; `grant_key` now takes the run id) and `call` uses up exactly its own plan (`current_call_id`). Two concurrent identical calls can no longer share or steal a plan. (b) Fail closed: a call with no plan never goes live; in auto or live mode it is refused ("no approval was decided for this test"), only an explicit `mode: "replay"` (offline) runs unplanned. (c) The plan stores the connection id the account resolved to; `call` resolves again and refuses if it differs (a default that changed after approval), and the key holds that id too. Tests: `concurrent_identical_tests_each_replay_and_an_unplanned_one_never_goes_live` (counts requests at a mock server), `a_default_account_that_changes_after_approval_stops_the_call`.
- **Review round 3**: (a) A script's sub-calls now run as calls of their own (`with_call(&call.id, ...)` in the script `exec`, each parallel call carrying its own id), so any tool that plans per call finds the plan made for that sub-call id. Note `connector_test` itself is in `NOT_IN_SCRIPTS`: a python script can never call it (with or without `[python] allow_writes`), so the reported "connector_test refused from scripts" could not happen; the fix is for the tools scripts can call that plan per call, and is tested with a stand-in planning tool (gather of three identical calls under one run grant, each finding its own plan, none unplanned). (b) Plans no longer accumulate: `Tool::forget_plans(run, call?)` (registry `forget_plans`) is called when a call won't run (denied, timed out, deferred, forbidden or read-only refused, in the loop and in scripts), after `diverged()`'s effect-only policy probe, and for the whole run when `drive` ends however it ended (completed, failed, cancelled). Tests: `a_denied_call_and_an_ended_run_leave_no_plans` (direct and script denials, read-only refusal, cancel mid-approval) and, for `connector_test`, `plans_of_calls_that_do_not_run_and_of_ended_runs_are_dropped`.
- **Not done**: run/conversation grants are not rebuilt from the journal on resume after a restart (the next call asks again); only `connector_test` names a key so far (other tools can opt in with `grant_key`); channel-timeline (non-Home) notes group only where they appear as transcript items.
- **Tests**: `tern-core` `agent::tests::grants` (run scope only that key, conversation scope across runs but not other conversations, always stores `key:` and never crosses tools or targets, destructive and tainted offer no grant, readonly still refuses), `tern-lua` `a_networked_drafts_test_names_a_grant_key_for_exactly_what_was_shown`, `daemon::manager::approval_card_tests` (sharing, release rules, abort handling), `tests/grants_e2e.rs` (the real daemon: run grant stops repeat prompts, a different tool still asks, `session` accepted, `always` stored as `key:`), the TUI scoped-prompt test, `tern_client` `models_test`, and desktop `test/approval_grants_test.dart` (card choices, grouping, the grouped row).

## Provider presets and the OpenRouter 404 (provider setup, revisited)

The original provider-setup scope (Anthropic and OpenAI-compatible only, no named presets) was revisited for ease of use. Spec section 12 in `docs/provider-setup-spec.md`.

- **Root cause of `HTTP 404: {"error":{"message":"Not Found","code":404}}` on every OpenRouter run (observed).** The user's `config.toml` had `base_url = "https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/responses"`: they pasted OpenRouter's OpenAI *Responses API* endpoint as the base address. tern's OpenAI-compatible provider uses Chat Completions and appends `/chat/completions`, so it called `.../api/v1/responses/chat/completions` and OpenRouter answered with its bare route-level 404. (Checked against a local server that answers like OpenRouter: only `/api/v1/chat/completions` exists, everything else gets that 404.) It got saved because a 404 from `GET {base}/models` counted as "key accepted" (`ListError::key_accepted`), and the `/v1` retry is skipped when the URL already ends in a version-looking segment; the first message was the first time anything exercised the address. A URL like `https://openrouter.ai/v1` fails the same way.
- **Fixes.** A 404 on the model list is now an error ("didn't recognise that address"), so a wrong address is never saved. `tern_core::presets::normalize_base_url` tidies addresses (drops a pasted endpoint path: `/chat/completions`, `/completions`, `/responses`, `/messages`, `/embeddings` or `/models`, keeping the `/api/v1` that belongs to the base; for a service in the preset list, replaces a bare host or guessed version path with the real one) and is applied when saving, when listing models and when building the provider, so an already-saved bad `openrouter.ai/v1` starts working with no config edit. OpenRouter calls carry `HTTP-Referer` and `X-Title: tern` (its optional app headers). `tern doctor` fails (not warns) on a 404.
- **Presets** (`tern_core::presets`, served at `GET /v1/providers/presets`, used by the TUI and CLI directly): Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenRouter, Google Gemini (its OpenAI-compatible endpoint), Groq, Together AI, Ollama, LM Studio, Other. A preset is not a provider kind and is not stored: it prefills kind, name, base URL and key hints, and what is saved is an ordinary `[providers.<name>]`. A configured provider is matched back to a preset by host (`ProviderStatus.preset`, `label`) for display and badges. `ProviderDraft.preset` lets the daemon fill what a client leaves out. Existing configs, including `openrouter:`-prefixed models, are unchanged.
- **Model picking never needs `provider:model` typed.** Pickers list `/models`, show the provider as a badge, and take a typed id under "Enter a model id". With exactly one provider configured, a typed id that no provider lists resolves to that provider (with the usual "not in its list" warning); with several, the desktop asks which. A preset's short `suggested_models` list shows (labelled "suggested") only when the provider's list can't be fetched; they are ids, with no price or capability claims, and may go stale.
- **Friendly call errors.** `tern_core::models::friendly_call_error` (and the Dart port `friendlyCallError`) maps `HTTP 401/402/403/404/429/5xx` and `network error:` to a sentence naming the provider, plus whether the fix is in Models. The raw text stays visible: the desktop row has "Show details", the TUI and `tern run` print it in parentheses. The mapping is on the error text rather than a new event field so the run journal and agent loop are untouched.
